CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    a = capacity_a
    b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    
    for gate, amount in events:
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            new = a + refill
            a = min(new, capacity_a)
        else:
            new = b + refill
            b = min(new, capacity_b)
        
        if gate == 'A':
            cost = amount
            if a >= cost:
                a -= cost
                output.append(amount)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                if gate == 'A':
                    tax_b += 1
        else:
            cost = amount + tax_b
            if b >= cost:
                b -= cost
                output.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
    
    return output

So the code:

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[int, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    a = capacity_a
    b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            new_val = a + refill
            a = min(new_val, capacity_a)
            cost = amount
            if a >= cost:
                a -= cost
                output.append(amount)
                if gate == 'B':
                    tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b = max(tax_b + 1, 0)
        elif gate == 'B':
            new_val = b + refill
            b = min(new_val, capacity_b)
            cost = amount + tax_b
            if b >= cost:
                b -= cost
                output.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                # tax_b remains the same
    return output

Wait, but in the code, for the 'A' gate's 'else' clause, the tax_b is increased. For 'B's, nothing is done to tax_b, which is correct.

CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[int, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    a = capacity_a
    b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        
        if gate == 'A':
            new_val = a + refill
            a = min(new_val, capacity_a)
            cost = amount
            if a >= cost:
                a -= cost
                output.append(amount)
                if gate == 'B':
                    tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b = max(tax_b + 1, 0)
        elif gate == 'B':
            new_val = b + refill
            b = min(new_val, capacity_b)
            cost = amount + tax_b
            if b >= cost:
                b -= cost
                output.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
    
    return output

THINK BLOCK:
Okay, I need to implement the dual_gate_ledger function in Python. Let's read the problem statement carefully to understand what needs to be done.

So, the problem involves two buckets, A and B. Both start full at their respective capacities. There's a global_denied counter which starts at 0 and increments when a request is denied. This affects the refill for both buckets. Additionally, there's a tax_b that increases by 1 each time a request on A is denied. Each successful B request costs an extra tax_b, and each successful B request then reduces tax_b by 1, but not below zero. Gate A is never taxed.

The function processes each event in order. Each event is a tuple of (gate, amount). The steps for each event are as follows:

1. Calculate the refill as base_refill plus the current global_denied.
2. Add this refill to the respective bucket (A or B), but cap it at the bucket's capacity. So bucket[gate] = min(capacity[gate], bucket[gate] + refill).
3. Determine the cost: if it's gate A, the cost is the amount. If it's gate B, the cost is amount plus the current tax_b.
4. Check if the bucket's current amount is >= cost. If yes:
   - Subtract the cost from the bucket.
   - Append the original amount to the output list.
   - If the gate is B, reduce tax_b by 1, but not below zero.
5. If the bucket is not enough (cost > current):
   - global_denied increments by 1.
   - If the denied gate is A, tax_b increases by 1. If it's B, tax_b doesn't change, because the tax is only applied to A's denial.

Hmm, so some key points to note here:

- The refill is based on the current global_denied before the step. So for each event, the refill is base_refill plus whatever global_denied was before processing this event.

- For each event, after processing, if it's successful, we update the buckets, output the allowed amount, and possibly adjust the tax_b. If it's denied, only the global_denied increases, and possibly tax_b if the denied gate was A.

So, to model this, I need to track the following variables:

- The current amounts in buckets A and B. Let's call them a and b, both initialized to their capacities.

- The global_denied counter, which starts at 0.

- The tax_b, which starts at 0.

- The output list, which collects the allowed amounts in order.

For each event in events:

Step 1: Refill = base_refill + global_denied.

Step 2: Update the bucket for that gate. For example, if the gate is A, new_a = min(capacity_a, a + refill). Similarly for B.

Wait, but capacity is capacity_a for A and capacity_b for B. So for each event, whether it's A or B, the refill is added, but it's capped at their respective capacities.

Yes.

Then, step 3: Compute cost. If the gate is B, cost = amount + tax_b. For A, it's just the amount.

Step 4: Check if the bucket has enough to cover cost.

If yes:

- Subtract cost from the bucket.
- Append the original amount to the output.
- If the gate is B, tax_b = max(0, tax_b - 1).

If no:

- global_denied += 1.
- If the gate is A, tax_b += 1.

So, let's model this.

Implementing this.

So the code will loop over each event, process as follows.

We'll create variables:

current_a, current_b = capacity_a, capacity_b

global_denied =0

tax_b=0

output=[]

for gate, amount in events:

   refill = base_refill + global_denied

   if gate == 'A':

       current_a += refill

       current_a = min(current_a, capacity_a)

   elif gate == 'B':

       current_b += refill

       current_b = min(current_b, capacity_b)

   # compute cost

   if gate == 'A':

       cost = amount

   else:

       cost = amount + tax_b

   # check if current >= cost

   if (gate == 'A' and current_a >= cost) or (gate == 'B' and current_b >= cost):

       # allowed

       if gate == 'A':

           current_a -= cost

       else:

           current_b -= cost

       output.append(amount)

       if gate == 'B':

           tax_b = max(0, tax_b -1)

   else:

       # denied

       global_denied +=1

       if gate == 'A':

           tax_b +=1

       # else, gate B, no change

So, the code is:

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    current_a = capacity_a
    current_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for gate, amount in events:
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            current_a += refill
            current_a = min(current_a, capacity_a)
            cost = amount
        else:
            current_b += refill
            current_b = min(current_b, capacity_b)
            cost = amount + tax_b
        if (gate == 'A' and current_a >= cost) or (gate == 'B' and current_b >= cost):
            if gate == 'A':
                current_a -= cost
            else:
                current_b -= cost
            output.append(amount)
            if gate == 'B':
                tax_b = max(0, tax_b - 1)
        else:
            global_denied += 1
            if gate == 'A':
                tax_b += 1
    return output

CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    current_a = capacity_a
    current_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    
    for gate, amount in events:
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        
        if gate == 'A':
            current_a += refill
            current_a = min(current_a, capacity_a)
            cost = amount
        else:
            current_b += refill
            current_b = min(current_b, capacity_b)
            cost = amount + tax_b
        
        if (gate == 'A' and current_a >= cost) or (gate == 'B' and current_b >= cost):
            if gate == 'A':
                current_a -= cost
            else:
                current_b -= cost
            output.append(amount)
            if gate == 'B':
                tax_b = max(0, tax_b - 1)
        else:
            global_denied += 1
            if gate == 'A':
                tax_b += 1
    
    return output

Okay, I need to implement the function dual_gate_ledger. Let's read the problem statement carefully to understand what needs to be done.

The problem involves two independent buckets, A and B. Both start full at their capacities. There's a global_denied counter which starts at 0 and increases every time a request on either gate is denied. The base_refill is added to refill each bucket based on this counter.

There's a cross-gate tax: each time a request on gate A is denied, tax_b increases by 1. When a successful withdrawal from B happens, tax_b decreases by 1, but not below zero. Gate A is never taxed.

The process for each event in the events list is as follows:

1. Calculate the refill as base_refill plus the current global_denied.
2. Add this refill to the respective bucket.
3. Subtract the requested amount (plus any tax for B) from the bucket.
4. If the bucket has enough, record the original amount and adjust the buckets and other variables. If B's bucket, reduce tax_b by 1. If not enough, increment global_denied and, if the denied gate is A, increase tax_b by 1.

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    a = capacity_a
    b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for gate, amount in events:
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            new_a = min(a + refill, capacity_a)
            a = new_a
            cost = amount
        else:
            new_b = min(b + refill, capacity_b)
            b = new_b
            cost = amount + tax_b
        if (gate == 'A' and a >= cost) or (gate == 'B' and b >= cost):
            if gate == 'B':
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            output.append(amount)
        else:
            global_denied += 1
            if gate == 'A':
                tax_b += 1
    return output

CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    a = capacity_a
    b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    
    for gate, amount in events:
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            new_a = min(a + refill, capacity_a)
            a = new_a
            cost = amount
        else:
            new_b = min(b + refill, capacity_b)
            b = new_b
            cost = amount + tax_b
        
        if (gate == 'A' and a >= cost) or (gate == 'B' and b >= cost):
            if gate == 'B':
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            output.append(amount)
        else:
            global_denied += 1
            if gate == 'A':
                tax_b += 1
    
    return output

Let's define the function.

bucket_a = capacity_a

bucket_b = capacity_b

global_denied =0

tax_b =0

output = []

for each event in events:

   gate, amount = event

   refill = base_refill + global_denied

   if gate == 'A':

       bucket_a += refill

       bucket_a = min(bucket_a, capacity_a)

   else:

       bucket_b += refill

       bucket_b = min(bucket_b, capacity_b)

   if gate == 'A':

       cost = amount

       if bucket_a >= cost:

           bucket_a -= cost

           output.append(amount)

       else:

           global_denied +=1

           tax_b = max(tax_b +1, 0)

   else: # gate is 'B'

       cost = amount + tax_b

       if bucket_b >= cost:

           bucket_b -= cost

           output.append(amount)

           tax_b = max(tax_b -1, 0)

       else:

           global_denied +=1

           # tax_b remains same

return output.

Now, implement in Python.

Wait, in Python, functions can't modify variables in outer scopes unless they are declared nonlocal, but in this case, we can just process as per code.

Wait, no. Because the function is to be written as a standalone function.

So, in code:

def dual_gate_ledger(events, capacity_a, capacity_b, base_refill):

    bucket_a = capacity_a

    bucket_b = capacity_b

    global_denied = 0

    tax_b = 0

    output = []

    for event in events:

        gate, amount = event

        refill = base_refill + global_denied

        if gate == 'A':

            bucket_a += refill

            bucket_a = min(bucket_a, capacity_a)

        else:

            bucket_b += refill

            bucket_b = min(bucket_b, capacity_b)

        if gate == 'A':

            # Process A

            if bucket_a >= amount:

                bucket_a -= amount

                output.append(amount)

            else:

                # deny

                global_denied +=1

                tax_b = max(tax_b + 1, 0)

        else:

            # Process B

            cost = amount + tax_b

            if bucket_b >= cost:

                bucket_b -= cost

                output.append(amount)

                tax_b = max(tax_b -1, 0)

            else:

                # deny

                global_denied +=1

                # tax_b remains same

    return output

CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events, capacity_a, capacity_b, base_refill):
    bucket_a = capacity_a
    bucket_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        
        if gate == 'A':
            bucket_a += refill
            bucket_a = min(bucket_a, capacity_a)
        else:
            bucket_b += refill
            bucket_b = min(bucket_b, capacity_b)
        
        if gate == 'A':
            if bucket_a >= amount:
                bucket_a -= amount
                output.append(amount)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b = max(tax_b + 1, 0)
        else:
            cost = amount + tax_b
            if bucket_b >= cost:
                bucket_b -= cost
                output.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
    
    return output

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    bucket_a = capacity_a
    bucket_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            bucket_a = min(bucket_a + refill, capacity_a)
        else:
            bucket_b = min(bucket_b + refill, capacity_b)
        cost = amount
        if gate == 'B':
            cost += tax_b
        if bucket_a if gate == 'A' else bucket_b >= cost:
            output.append(amount)
            if gate == 'B':
                tax_b = max(tax_b -1, 0)
            if gate == 'A':
                pass  # no tax_b change
            else:
                pass  # no tax_b change
            # subtract cost from bucket
            if gate == 'A':
                bucket_a -= cost
            else:
                bucket_b -= cost
        else:
            global_denied +=1
            if gate == 'A':
                tax_b +=1
    return output

if bucket_a if gate == 'A' else bucket_b >= cost.

if (gate == 'A' and bucket_a >= cost) or (gate == 'B' and bucket_b >= cost):

Which is correct.

But in Python, it's written as (bucket_a if gate == 'A' else bucket_b) >= cost.

Wait, no, because if gate is 'A', it returns bucket_a, else bucket_b. So if that value is >= cost, then allowed.

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    bucket_a = capacity_a
    bucket_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            bucket_a = min(bucket_a + refill, capacity_a)
        else:
            bucket_b = min(bucket_b + refill, capacity_b)
        cost = amount
        if gate == 'B':
            cost += tax_b
        if gate == 'A' and bucket_a >= cost:
            output.append(amount)
            tax_b  # no change for A
            bucket_a -= cost
        elif gate == 'B' and bucket_b >= cost:
            output.append(amount)
            tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            bucket_b -= cost
        else:
            global_denied += 1
            if gate == 'A':
                tax_b += 1
    return output

            tax_b +=1

    else:

        if bucket_b >= cost:

            output.append(amount)

            tax_b = max(tax_b -1, 0)

            bucket_b -= cost

        else:

            global_denied +=1

So, the code can be written as:

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    bucket_a = capacity_a
    bucket_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for gate, amount in events:
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            new_level = bucket_a + refill
            if new_level > capacity_a:
                new_level = capacity_a
            bucket_a = new_level
        else:
            new_level = bucket_b + refill
            if new_level > capacity_b:
                new_level = capacity_b
            bucket_b = new_level
        cost = amount
        if gate == 'B':
            cost += tax_b
        if gate == 'A':
            if bucket_a >= cost:
                output.append(amount)
                bucket_a -= cost
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b += 1
        else:  # B
            if bucket_b >= cost:
                output.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
                bucket_b -= cost
            else:
                global_denied += 1
    return output

Wait, but in the else part for B, when denied, do nothing to tax_b except when the gate is A.

This code structure may work.

CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    bucket_a = capacity_a
    bucket_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for gate, amount in events:
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            new_level = bucket_a + refill
            if new_level > capacity_a:
                new_level = capacity_a
            bucket_a = new_level
        else:
            new_level = bucket_b + refill
            if new_level > capacity_b:
                new_level = capacity_b
            bucket_b = new_level
        cost = amount
        if gate == 'B':
            cost += tax_b
        if gate == 'A':
            if bucket_a >= cost:
                output.append(amount)
                bucket_a -= cost
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b += 1
        else:
            if bucket_b >= cost:
                output.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
                bucket_b -= cost
            else:
                global_denied += 1
    return output

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    current_a = capacity_a
    current_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    result = []
    base = base_refill

        gate, amount = event

        if gate == 'A':

        else:

            # in either case, global_denied increases by 1 for 'A' or 'B' denial
            global_denied +=1
    return result

CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    current_a = capacity_a
    current_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    result = []
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        refill = base_refill + global_denied
        if gate == 'A':
            current_a = min(current_a + refill, capacity_a)
        else:
            current_b = min(current_b + refill, capacity_b)
        cost = amount
        if gate == 'B':
            cost += tax_b
        if gate == 'A':
            if current_a >= cost:
                current_a -= cost
                result.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(0, tax_b - 1)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b += 1
        else:
            if current_b >= cost:
                current_b -= cost
                result.append(amount)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b = max(0, tax_b - 1)
    return result

But why use max here? Because if tax_b was 0, subtracting 1 would make it negative. So, the max ensures it's at least 0.

      new_bucket = min(new_bucket, capacity_a)

          if gate == 'A':

      new_bucket = min(new_bucket, capacity_b)

- global_denied starts at 0.

    new_bucket_a = bucket_a + refill

    new_bucket_a = min(new_bucket_a, capacity_a)

    if new_bucket_a >= amount:

        bucket_a = new_bucket_a - amount

        output.append(amount)

    else:

        global_denied +=1

        # gate is 'A', so tax_b increases

        tax_b +=1

elif gate is 'B':

    refill = base_refill + global_denied

    new_bucket_b = bucket_b + refill

    new_bucket_b = min(new_bucket_b, capacity_b)

    cost = amount + tax_b

    if new_bucket_b >= cost:

        bucket_b = new_bucket_b - cost

        output.append(amount)

        tax_b = max(tax_b -1, 0)  # ensure not below zero

    else:

        global_denied +=1

        # no tax change for B's denial

Wait, but what if the cost is larger than new_bucket_b?

Yes.

Now, in code:

def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    bucket_a = capacity_a
    bucket_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        if gate == 'A':
            refill = base_refill + global_denied
            new_bucket_a = bucket_a + refill
            new_bucket_a = min(new_bucket_a, capacity_a)
            if new_bucket_a >= amount:
                bucket_a = new_bucket_a - amount
                output.append(amount)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b += 1
        elif gate == 'B':
            refill = base_refill + global_denied
            new_bucket_b = bucket_b + refill
            new_bucket_b = min(new_bucket_b, capacity_b)
            cost = amount + tax_b
            if new_bucket_b >= cost:
                bucket_b = new_bucket_b - cost
                output.append(amount)
                # decrease tax_b, but not below 0
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
    return output

Wait, but in the fifth test case, the code now correctly returns [10,1], because for the 'A' event3, the new_bucket is 1, which is less than 10, so it's denied, leading to the global_denied increasing and tax_b increasing.

So, the code should now handle all cases correctly.

Testing it against the fifth test case:

After event3, 'A',10:

global_denied is 2, tax_b is 2.

event4: 'B',1.

refill is 0+2=2.

new_bucket_b=10+2=12 →10.

cost is 1+2=3.

10 >=3 → allowed.

bucket_b is 7, and tax_b is 2-1=1.

output is [10,1].

Which is correct.

Thus, the code should pass the test cases.

CODE:
def dual_gate_ledger(events: list[tuple[str, int]], capacity_a: int, capacity_b: int, base_refill: int) -> list[int]:
    bucket_a = capacity_a
    bucket_b = capacity_b
    global_denied = 0
    tax_b = 0
    output = []
    
    for event in events:
        gate, amount = event
        
        if gate == 'A':
            refill = base_refill + global_denied
            new_bucket_a = bucket_a + refill
            new_bucket_a = min(new_bucket_a, capacity_a)
            
            if new_bucket_a >= amount:
                bucket_a = new_bucket_a - amount
                output.append(amount)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
                tax_b += 1
        elif gate == 'B':
            refill = base_refill + global_denied
            new_bucket_b = bucket_b + refill
            new_bucket_b = min(new_bucket_b, capacity_b)
            
            cost = amount + tax_b
            if new_bucket_b >= cost:
                bucket_b = new_bucket_b - cost
                output.append(amount)
                tax_b = max(tax_b - 1, 0)
            else:
                global_denied += 1
    
    return output
